## Interpretation notes

- Scores are defect-risk/maintainability indicators and should be read with the marker type, file size, test presence, history, and architectural role.
- Rank cleanup candidates by weighted deficit and defect relevance, not score alone; a small file can have a low score while contributing fewer total risk points than a large file.
- Churn, change entropy, co-change, prior-defect, and ownership markers describe historical risk. They are not necessarily code defects to eliminate directly.
- Duplication markers can be noisy for fixtures, JSX, generated-looking structures, and intra-file repeated shapes; verify before refactoring.
- Performance markers are static signals. Validate whether the flagged path is actually latency-sensitive before changing it.

## Report-generation commands

The report was assembled from these read-only or index-analysis commands:

- `repowise health --format json --no-workspace`
- `repowise health --refactoring-targets --format json --no-workspace`
- `repowise dead-code --include-internals --min-confidence 0 --format json --no-workspace`
- `repowise doctor --format json --no-workspace`
- Repowise MCP `get_health(include=["refactoring"], only=["refactoring_plans"], limit=50)`

The source checkout was at commit `956e95f9c75e` when the index was refreshed and the report was generated.
